```json
  "id": "Topielec atau utopiec adalah roh air Slavia, jiwa orang yang mati tenggelam, yang menyeret manusia ke rawa dan danau.",

   "id": "Topielec, juga disebut utopiec, vodník, atau topnik, adalah nama bagi roh air dalam kepercayaan Slavia. Utopce adalah jiwa manusia yang mati tenggelam dan tetap tinggal di air tempat mereka meninggal. Mereka dipercaya menyeret orang ke rawa dan danau serta membunuh hewan yang berdiri di dekat air tenang.",
   "en": "Topielec, also called utopiec, vodník or topnik, is a name for water spirits in Slavic belief. The utopce are the souls of people who drowned and remain in the water where they died. They are believed to drag people into swamps and lakes and to kill animals standing near still water.",

   "quote": "Utopiec (plural Utopce), Vodník or Topnik is a name applied to Slavic spirits of water.    "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Utopiec (jamak utopce), Vodník, atau Topnik adalah nama roh air Slavia; utopce adalah jiwa manusia yang mati tenggelam dan tinggal di air tempat mereka mati, menyeret orang ke rawa dan danau serta membunuh hewan di dekat air tenang.",

  {

   "quote": "O topielce são espíritos de almas humanas que morreram se afogando, residindo no elemento de seu próprio falecimento.",

   "context": "traditional-belief",

  {
   "id": "topielec-c04",
   "source_id": "topielec-s1",
   "quote": "Slavonic water spirits of the drowned dead remained a popular element of rural Polish folklore at the turn of the 19th and 20th century, as shown by Władysław Reymont in his Nobel Prize-winning novel Chłopi (The Peasants).",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Roh air Slavia dari orang yang mati tenggelam tetap populer dalam cerita rakyat pedesaan Polandia pada pergantian abad ke-19 dan ke-20 (19th, 20th), seperti ditunjukkan Władysław Reymont dalam novel Chłopi (Para Petani) yang meraih Hadiah Nobel.",
    "en": "Slavic water spirits of the drowned remained popular in rural Polish folklore at the turn of the 19th and 20th century, as Władysław Reymont showed in his Nobel Prize-winning novel Chłopi (The Peasants)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "topielec-c05",
   "source_id": "topielec-s1",

   "context": "modern-popular-culture",

    "en": "A more recent example in Polish popular culture is the comic series Lil and Put (Lil i Put), whose two title characters are constantly at odds with an utopiec in a pond next to their village."
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Zână adalah peri dalam mitologi Rumania, padanan peri ibu baptis, yang tinggal di hutan dan membantu orang baik.",
  "en": "The zână is a fairy of Romanian mythology, akin to the fairy godmother, who lives in the woods and helps good people.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "zana-c01",
   "zana-c03"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam tradisi Rumania, zână (jamak zâne) setara dengan Charites Yunani atau peri ibu baptis, dan merupakan kebalikan dari monster seperti Muma Pădurii. Mereka tampil sebagai tokoh baik dalam dongeng dan kebanyakan tinggal di hutan. Ukuran dan rupanya beragam, dan mereka dapat berubah wujud agar menyatu dengan lingkungan untuk berlindung.",
   "en": "In Romanian tradition the zână (plural zâne) corresponds to the Greek Charites or the fairy godmother and is the opposite of monsters such as Muma Pădurii. They appear as good figures in fairy tales and mostly live in the woods. They vary in size and look and can change form to blend into their surroundings for protection.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "zana-c01",
    "zana-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Zâne memberi kehidupan kepada janin dalam kandungan dan menganugerahkan bakat seperti menari, kecantikan, kebaikan hati, dan keberuntungan. Namun kebaikan mereka ada batasnya: orang yang melanggar pantangan, misalnya masuk ke wilayah zâne tanpa izin, bisa dihukum dengan kebutaan.",
   "en": "Zâne give life to babies still in the womb and grant them talents such as dancing, beauty, kindness and luck. Their goodwill has limits, though: anyone who breaks a taboo, for instance by entering zâne land uninvited, may be punished with blindness.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "zana-c03",
    "zana-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Zâne terbagi menjadi baik dan jahat; zâne yang baik adalah gadis muda yang cantik dan selalu punya jalan keluar bagi sang pahlawan, biasanya Făt-Frumos. Zâne yang baik dipercaya lahir dari bunga.",
   "en": "Zâne are divided into good and evil ones; the good zâne are beautiful young maidens who always hold the solution for the hero, usually Făt-Frumos. Good zâne are believed to be born from flowers.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "zana-c06",
    "zana-c07",
    "zana-c08"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "zana-c01",
   "source_id": "zana-s1",
   "quote": "is the Romanian equivalent of the Greek Charites or the fairy godmother. They are the opposite of monsters such as Muma Pădurii. These characters make positive appearances in fairy tales and mostly reside in the woods.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zână adalah padanan Rumania untuk Charites Yunani atau peri ibu baptis, kebalikan dari monster seperti Muma Pădurii; mereka tampil positif dalam dongeng dan kebanyakan tinggal di hutan.",
    "en": "The zână is the Romanian equivalent of the Greek Charites or the fairy godmother, the opposite of monsters like Muma Pădurii; they appear positively in fairy tales and mostly live in the woods."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zana-c02",
   "source_id": "zana-s1",
   "quote": "They vary in size and appearance and can transform to blend into their surroundings for protection and cover. They can appear openly in the woods and coax travelers to follow them in order to help them find their way.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ukuran dan rupa mereka beragam dan dapat berubah wujud untuk menyatu dengan lingkungan demi perlindungan; mereka dapat muncul di hutan dan membujuk pengelana mengikuti mereka agar menemukan jalan.",
    "en": "They vary in size and look and can transform to blend into their surroundings for protection; they may appear in the woods and coax travellers to follow them to find their way."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zana-c03",
   "source_id": "zana-s1",
   "quote": "They give life to fetuses in utero and bestow upon them great gifts like the art of dancing, beauty, kindness, and luck.",
   "locator": "Mythological role",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka memberi kehidupan kepada janin dalam kandungan dan menganugerahkan bakat seperti menari, kecantikan, kebaikan hati, dan keberuntungan.",
    "en": "They give life to unborn children and bestow gifts like dancing, beauty, kindness and luck."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zana-c04",
   "source_id": "zana-s2",
   "quote": "Totuși zânele bune au limite în bunăvoință și uneori pedepsesc îndeosebi pe cel care le calcă un tabu declarat (de exemplu, intrarea neîngăduită pe un teritoriu controlat de zâne), pedeapsa curentă fiind orbirea.",
   "locator": "Natură",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Namun kebaikan zâne ada batasnya: kadang mereka menghukum orang yang melanggar pantangan, misalnya masuk tanpa izin ke wilayah zâne, dengan hukuman yang lazim berupa kebutaan.",
    "en": "Yet the good zâne's kindness has limits: they sometimes punish whoever breaks a declared taboo, such as entering zâne territory uninvited, the usual penalty being blindness."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zana-c05",
   "source_id": "zana-s1",
   "quote": "The word zână comes from the Roman goddess Diana (as does Astur-Leonese xana).",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kata zână berasal dari nama dewi Romawi Diana (begitu pula xana dalam bahasa Astur-Leon).",
    "en": "The word zână comes from the Roman goddess Diana (as does Astur-Leonese xana)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zana-c06",
   "source_id": "zana-s2",
   "quote": "În mitologia românească, acestea sunt de 2 categorii morale: bune și rele. Zânele bune sunt preponderente numeric și categorial, sunt fecioare frumoase, zvelte, foarte tinere, năzdrăvane, prielnice omului cinstit și având totdeauna soluțiile esențiale pentru dificultățile ori impasurile inevitabile de care este asaltat eroul arhetipal (de obicei Făt-Frumos).",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Rumania, zâne terbagi menjadi baik dan jahat; zâne yang baik lebih banyak, berupa gadis cantik yang sangat muda dan sakti, berpihak pada orang jujur, dan selalu punya jalan keluar bagi pahlawan, biasanya Făt-Frumos.",
    "en": "In Romanian mythology zâne are of two moral kinds, good and evil; the good ones predominate, being beautiful, very young, magical maidens who favour honest people and always hold the solution for the hero, usually Făt-Frumos."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zana-c07",
   "source_id": "zana-s2",
   "quote": "Zânele bune sunt socotite ca născându-se din flori.",
   "locator": "Natură",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zâne yang baik dipercaya lahir dari bunga.",
    "en": "Good zâne are thought to be born from flowers."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zana-c08",
   "source_id": "zana-s2",
   "quote": "Acestea sunt adesea confundate cu Ielele, însă prin contaminare, căci  sunt divinități structural și atributiv diferite.",
   "locator": "Natură",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zâne jahat sering dikacaukan dengan Ielele, padahal keduanya makhluk ilahi yang berbeda.",
    "en": "Evil zâne are often confused with the Ielele, though they are structurally different divinities."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {
   "field": "images",
   "searched": "Tidak ada gambar Commons yang terbukti menggambarkan zână."
  }
 ]
}
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Bukavac adalah makhluk iblis dalam mitologi Slavia dari Syrmia, monster berkaki enam yang keluar dari danau pada malam hari untuk mencekik manusia dan hewan.",
  "en": "Bukavac is a demonic creature of Slavic mythology from Syrmia, a six-legged monster that leaves lakes at night to strangle people and animals.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "bukavac-c01",
   "bukavac-c02"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Bukavac adalah makhluk mitis bersifat iblis dalam mitologi Slavia; kepercayaan kepadanya ada di Syrmia. Ia kadang dibayangkan sebagai monster berkaki enam bertanduk bengkok dengan mata biru terang.",
   "en": "Slavic myth knows the bukavac as a demonic being, and people in Syrmia believed in it. Some pictured it with six legs, twisted horns and shining blue eyes.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bukavac-c01",
    "bukavac-c02",
    "bukavac-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia tinggal di danau dan kolam, keluar dari air pada malam hari sambil membuat suara gaduh, yang menjadi asal namanya dari kata buka, \"kebisingan\". Ia melompat ke atas manusia dan hewan lalu mencekik mereka. Dalam bahasa Serbia-Kroasia ia juga disebut \"banteng air\" (vodeni bik).",
   "en": "It lives in lakes and pools and leaves the water at night making a loud noise, which gives it its name from buka, \"noise\". It jumps on people and animals and strangles them. It is also called the \"water bull\" (vodeni bik).",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bukavac-c02",
    "bukavac-c03",
    "bukavac-c04"
   ]
  }
 ],
 "cultural_context": null,

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "bukavac-c01",
   "source_id": "bukavac-s1",
   "quote": "is a demonic mythical creature in Slavic mythology. Belief in it existed in Syrmia.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bukavac adalah makhluk mitis bersifat iblis dalam mitologi Slavia; kepercayaan kepadanya ada di Syrmia.",
    "en": "Bukavac is a demonic mythical creature of Slavic mythology; belief in it existed in Syrmia."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bukavac-c02",
   "source_id": "bukavac-s1",
   "quote": "Bukavac was sometimes imagined as a six-legged monster with gnarled horns and bright blue eyes.  It lives in lakes and pools, coming out of the water during the night to make a loud noise (hence the name: buka – noise). It would jump onto people and animals and strangle them.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bukavac kadang dibayangkan sebagai monster berkaki enam bertanduk bengkok dan bermata biru terang; ia tinggal di danau dan kolam, keluar pada malam hari dengan suara gaduh (dari buka, kebisingan), melompat ke manusia dan hewan lalu mencekiknya.",
    "en": "Bukavac was sometimes imagined as a six-legged monster with gnarled horns and bright blue eyes; it lives in lakes and pools, comes out at night making a loud noise (buka, noise), and jumps on people and animals to strangle them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bukavac-c03",
   "source_id": "bukavac-s2",
   "quote": "Бу́кавац или Водени бик, воденбика (сербохорв. Букавац, Bukavac «шумящий») — демоническое существо мифологии, похожее на оборотня (вампира). Верования в него зафиксированы в Среме (Сербия, Хорватия).",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bukavac atau Vodeni bik (\"banteng air\"; Bukavac berarti \"yang bergemuruh\") adalah makhluk iblis mirip manusia serigala atau vampir; kepercayaan kepadanya tercatat di Syrmia (Serbia, Kroasia).",
    "en": "Bukavac or Vodeni bik (\"water bull\"; Bukavac meaning \"the noisy one\") is a demonic being resembling a werewolf or vampire; belief in it is recorded in Syrmia (Serbia, Croatia)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bukavac-c04",
   "source_id": "bukavac-s1",
   "quote": "It would jump onto people and animals and strangle them.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia melompat ke atas manusia dan hewan lalu mencekik mereka.",
    "en": "It would jump on people and animals and strangle them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bukavac-c05",
   "source_id": "bukavac-s2",
   "quote": "Букавац иногда описывается, как шестиногий монстр с оленьими рогами.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bukavac kadang digambarkan sebagai monster berkaki enam bertanduk rusa.",
    "en": "Bukavac is sometimes described as a six-legged monster with deer antlers."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bukavac-c06",
   "source_id": "bukavac-s2",
   "quote": "Букавац, Буковац, Букало — иносказательно: человек производящий много шума.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Secara kiasan, bukavac atau bukalo berarti orang yang sangat berisik.",
    "en": "Figuratively, bukavac or bukalo means a person who makes a lot of noise."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Chernava adalah putri (atau keponakan) Tsar Laut dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, putri duyung yang menjadi roh dan perwujudan sungai bernama sama, terkenal dari bylina Sadko.",
  "en": "Chernava is the daughter (or niece) of the Sea Tsar in Russian folklore, a mermaid who is the spirit and embodiment of the river of the same name, famed from the bylina of Sadko.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "chernava-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, Chernava adalah putri Tsar Laut, atau dalam beberapa versi keponakannya, sekaligus roh dan perwujudan sungai yang bernama sama. Ia putri duyung: kepala dan tubuh atasnya manusia, sedangkan tubuh bawahnya ekor ikan.",
   "en": "Russian folk tradition makes Chernava the daughter, or in some tellings the niece, of the Sea Tsar, and the living spirit of the river named after her. Above the waist she is a woman; below, she has a fish's tail.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "chernava-c01"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam bylina Sadko, ia termasuk 900 putri duyung yang ditawarkan kepada Sadko sebagai pengantin; menikahinya berarti Sadko tidak bisa kembali ke dunia manusia. Sadko memilih Chernava tetapi tidak menyentuhnya pada malam pernikahan. Ketika Sadko tidur, Chernava berubah menjadi sungai dan membantunya kembali; Sadko terbangun di tepi sungai Chernava dan berkumpul lagi dengan istri pertamanya.",
   "en": "In the Sadko bylina she is one of the 900 mermaids offered to Sadko as a bride; consummating the marriage would keep him from the human world. Sadko chose Chernava but did not touch her on the wedding night. While he slept she turned into a river and helped him return; he woke on the bank of the river Chernava and rejoined his first wife.",

   "quote": "is Sea Tsar's daughter (or, according to some versions, a niece), spirit and personification of the river of the same name. She is a mermaid. Her head and upper body are human, while the lower body is a fish's tail. Chernava is famed from her appearance in the epic of Sadko.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, Chernava (Чернава) adalah putri Tsar Laut (atau keponakannya), roh dan perwujudan sungai bernama sama; ia putri duyung berkepala dan bertubuh atas manusia dengan ekor ikan, terkenal dari epos Sadko.",

   "source_id": "chernava-s1",
   "quote": "In the Sadko bylina, Chernava appears as one of the 900 mermaids offered to Sadko as a new bride, though to consummate the marriage would mean that Sadko would no longer be able to go to the human world.",
   "locator": "In Sadko",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam bylina Sadko, Chernava adalah salah satu dari 900 putri duyung yang ditawarkan kepada Sadko sebagai pengantin; menyempurnakan pernikahan berarti Sadko tak bisa kembali ke dunia manusia.",
    "en": "In the Sadko bylina Chernava is one of 900 mermaids offered to Sadko as a bride; consummating the marriage would mean he could not return to the human world."

  },
  {
   "id": "chernava-c03",
   "source_id": "chernava-s1",
   "quote": "On their wedding night he did not touch her. When Sadko was asleep, Chernava had transformed into a river, helping him to get into the human world. Sadko woke up on the shore of the river Chernava and rejoined his first wife.",
   "locator": "In Sadko",

  {
   "id": "chernava-c05",
   "source_id": "chernava-s2",
   "quote": "В честь Чернавы названы холмы на Венере — «холмы Чернавы», утверждённые в 1997 году.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bukit di Venus, \"Bukit Chernava\", dinamai untuk menghormatinya dan disahkan pada tahun 1997.",
    "en": "Hills on Venus, the \"Chernava hills\", are named in her honour and were approved in 1997."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernava-c06",
   "source_id": "chernava-s2",
   "quote": "Красавица. Одна из 900 (точнее 901-я, «три по триста и ещё одна…») морских дев, самая последняя и прекрасная из них.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia gadis cantik, salah satu dari 900 gadis laut (tepatnya yang ke-901, \"tiga kali tiga ratus dan satu lagi\"), yang terakhir dan tercantik di antara mereka.",
    "en": "A beauty, one of the 900 sea maidens (precisely the 901st, \"three times three hundred and one more\"), the last and most beautiful of them."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {
   "field": "images",

  }
 ]
}
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Habernitsa atau Cornflower Wraith adalah iblis tengah hari dalam mitologi Slavia dari daerah Prudnik, perempuan berbaju biru langit yang menghukum orang yang menginjak gandum.",
  "en": "Habernitsa, the Cornflower Wraith, is a noon demon of Slavic mythology from the Prudnik area, a woman in azure who punishes those who trample the grain.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Habernitsa adalah iblis tengah hari dalam mitologi Slavia yang dikatakan tinggal di sekitar Prudnik, di Silesia; dalam bahasa Polandia ia disebut chabernica. Ia mirip dengan Lady Midday (południca). Biasanya ia digambarkan sebagai perempuan muda ramping berbaju biru langit dengan bunga cornflower di rambutnya, yang berkeliaran di pematang ladang saat tengah hari.",
   "en": "Habernitsa is a noon demon of Slavic mythology said to live around Prudnik in Silesia; in Polish she is the chabernica. She resembles Lady Midday (południca). She was usually pictured as a slim young woman in azure with cornflowers in her hair, roaming the field edges at midday.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "cornflower-wraith-c01",
    "cornflower-wraith-c02",
    "cornflower-wraith-c04"

  },
  {
   "id": "Ia marah kepada orang yang menginjak-injak gandum atau memakai alat tajam. Orang yang dianggapnya pantas dihukum ditidurkan dengan bisikannya, lalu ditimpa sakit kepala, kelumpuhan, atau nyeri punggung bawah; kadang ia menyerang dengan mematahkan lengan, kaki, atau leher korbannya. Untuk menghindari amarahnya, pekerja harus beristirahat saat tengah hari ketika doa Angelus.",
   "en": "Trampling the grain or working with sharp tools roused her anger. Those she judged deserving were lulled to sleep by her whisper and then struck with headache, paralysis or low back pain; sometimes she broke her victims' arms, legs or neck. To avoid her wrath a worker had to rest at midday, at the Angelus.",

    "cornflower-wraith-c03"

   "en": "A dictionary of Polish dialects describes the chabernica as a bogey used to scare children away from the grain fields. There is also a story in which she freed a little girl caught in thorny brambles and led her home.",
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Etiäinen adalah sejenis haltija dalam cerita rakyat Finlandia, bayangan atau kembaran seseorang yang mendahuluinya dan melakukan hal yang kelak ia lakukan.",
  "en": "In Finnish folklore the etiäinen is a kind of haltija, an image or double of a person that goes ahead of them and does what they will later do.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "etiainen-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Finlandia, semua tempat, benda, dan juga manusia memiliki haltija, roh penjaga masing-masing. Salah satunya adalah etiäinen: bayangan, kembaran, atau sekadar kesan yang berjalan mendahului seseorang dan melakukan hal yang kelak dilakukan orang itu. Misalnya, orang di rumah mendengar pintu ditutup atau melihat bayangan orang yang mereka tunggu, lalu sadar ia belum datang; ketika ia benar-benar tiba, ia mengulangi tindakan etiäinen-nya.",
   "en": "In Finnish folklore every place and thing, and every person, has a haltija, a guardian spirit of its own. One of them is the etiäinen: an image, double or mere impression that goes ahead of a person and does what that person will later do. People waiting at home might hear the door close or see the shadow of the one they expect, then realise that person has not arrived; when they finally come, they repeat what their etiäinen did.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "etiainen-c01",
    "etiainen-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Etiäinen juga bisa berarti firasat bahwa sesuatu akan terjadi, misalnya peringatan akan datangnya tahun yang buruk. Dalam bahasa Finlandia modern, kata ini sudah lepas dari asal-usul syamanistiknya dan berarti firasat. Etiäiset muncul secara spontan, tidak dapat dipanggil, dan kadang tidak diinginkan serta menimbulkan kecemasan seperti hantu.",
   "en": "Etiäinen can also mean a feeling that something will happen, such as a warning of a bad year ahead. In modern Finnish the word has moved away from its shamanistic roots and means premonition. Etiäiset are spontaneous, cannot be summoned, and may be unwanted and cause anxiety, like ghosts.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "etiainen-c02",
    "etiainen-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Seseorang dipercaya mendapat etiäinen pada usia tiga hari, dan etiäinen itu mengikuti watak pemiliknya.",
   "en": "A person is said to receive their etiäinen at three days old, and it follows its owner's character traits.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "etiainen-c05"
   ]
  }
 ],
 "cultural_context": null,
 "etymology": null,

   ]
  },
  "origin": {

   "en": "It comes from Finnish folklore.",

   "en": "It signals someone's arrival before they come.",

   "id": "etiainen-c02",
   "source_id": "etiainen-s1",
   "quote": "Etiäinen can also refer to some kind of a feeling that something is going to happen. Sometimes it could, for example, warn of a bad year coming.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Etiäinen juga bisa berarti perasaan bahwa sesuatu akan terjadi, misalnya peringatan akan datangnya tahun yang buruk.",
    "en": "Etiäinen can also mean a feeling that something is going to happen, for example a warning of a bad year coming."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "etiainen-c03",
   "source_id": "etiainen-s1",
   "quote": "In modern Finnish, the term has detached from its shamanistic origins and refers to premonition. Unlike clairvoyance, divination, and similar practices, etiäiset (plural) are spontaneous and cannot be induced. Quite the opposite, they may be unwanted and cause anxiety, like ghosts.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam bahasa Finlandia modern istilah ini lepas dari asal syamanistiknya dan berarti firasat; etiäiset (jamak) bersifat spontan, tidak bisa dipanggil, dan dapat tidak diinginkan serta menimbulkan kecemasan seperti hantu.",
    "en": "In modern Finnish the term has left its shamanistic origins and means premonition; etiäiset (plural) are spontaneous, cannot be induced, and may be unwanted and cause anxiety, like ghosts."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "etiainen-c04",
   "source_id": "etiainen-s2",
   "quote": "Ihmiset saattavat kuulla esimerkiksi perheenjäsenensä kävelevän pihaan, avaavan oven, jopa nähdä tämän hahmon tai varjon, kunnes huomaavat, ettei tämä todellisuudessa vielä tullutkaan. Pian perheenjäsen kuitenkin tulee ja toistaa saman kuin etiäisensä.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang bisa mendengar anggota keluarganya masuk halaman dan membuka pintu, bahkan melihat sosok atau bayangannya, lalu sadar ia belum datang; tak lama ia benar-benar datang dan mengulangi apa yang dilakukan etiäinen-nya.",
    "en": "People may hear a family member walk into the yard and open the door, even see their figure or shadow, then realise they have not come; soon the person arrives and repeats what their etiäinen did."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "etiainen-c05",
   "source_id": "etiainen-s2",
   "quote": "Etiäinen saadaan kolmen päivän vanhana, ja se noudattaa ihmisensä luonteenpiirteitä.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Etiäinen diperoleh pada usia tiga hari dan mengikuti watak pemiliknya.",
    "en": "The etiäinen is received at three days old and follows its person's character traits."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "etiainen-c06",
   "source_id": "etiainen-s2",
   "quote": "Vardøger, norjalainen etiäinen",
   "locator": "Katso myös",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vardøger adalah etiäinen versi Norwegia.",
    "en": "Vardøger is the Norwegian etiäinen."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {
   "field": "images",
   "searched": "Tidak ada gambar Commons yang terbukti menggambarkan etiäinen."
  }
 ]
}
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Gagana adalah burung ajaib berparuh besi dan bercakar tembaga dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, yang tinggal di Pulau Buyan dan sering disebut dalam mantra.",
  "en": "In Russian folklore Gagana is a wondrous bird, iron-beaked and copper-clawed, who dwells on Buyan Island and turns up often in charms.",
  "claim_ids": [

  ]

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Gagana adalah burung ajaib dalam cerita rakyat Rusia yang berparuh besi dan bercakar tembaga. Ia dikatakan tinggal di Pulau Buyan dan sering disebut dalam mantra. Burung ini juga disebut menjaga batu Alatyr bersama ular Garafena.",
   "en": "Gagana is a miraculous bird of Russian folklore with an iron beak and copper claws. She is said to live on Buyan Island and is often named in incantations. The bird is also said to guard the Alatyr stone together with the snake Garafena.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "gagana-c01"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Gagana pandai menyihir dan membuat keajaiban, dan bila dimintai dengan benar dapat menolong manusia. Ia juga satu-satunya yang mampu memberikan \"susu burung\". Dalam mantra yang diterbitkan I. P. Sakharov, Gagana duduk di Buyan dan harus mengusir serta menggigit \"murii hitam\".",
   "en": "Gagana can conjure and work miracles and, if asked correctly, can help a person. She is also the only one able to give \"bird's milk\". In a charm published by I. P. Sakharov, Gagana sits on Buyan and is to drive off and bite the \"black murii\".",
   "claim_ids": [

  {
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Meduza atau Meluza adalah makhluk mitis dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, digambarkan pada lubok abad ke-17 atau ke-18 sebagai setengah perempuan setengah ular atau ikan.",
  "en": "Meduza or Meluza is a mythical creature of Russian folklore, shown in a 17th- or 18th-century lubok as half woman and half snake or fish.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "meduza-russian-folklore-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Meduza, juga disebut Meluza (\"yang kecil\") atau Meluzina, adalah makhluk mitis dalam cerita rakyat Rusia yang digambarkan pada lubok (gambar cetak rakyat) abad ke-17 atau ke-18. Ia dilukiskan sebagai setengah perempuan setengah ular, atau setengah perempuan setengah ikan, dan juga disebut dewi tipu daya.",
   "en": "Meduza, also called Meluza (\"little one\") or Meluzina, is a mythical creature of Russian folklore depicted in a lubok (popular print) of the 17th or 18th century. She is shown as half woman and half snake, or half woman and half fish, and is also said to be a deity of deception.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "meduza-russian-folklore-c01",
    "meduza-russian-folklore-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai monster laut berkepala gadis cantik berambut gelap dan bermahkota, bertubuh binatang belang, berekor naga yang ujungnya mulut ular, dan berkaki seperti gajah yang ujungnya juga mulut ular. Mulut-mulut ular itu dipercaya mengandung racun naga yang mematikan. Ia tinggal di laut dekat jurang Ethiopia atau di Samudra Barat.",
   "en": "The lubok shows a sea creature whose crowned head is that of a lovely dark-haired girl, set on a striped animal's body, with a dragon's tail ending in a snake's mouth, and elephant-like legs that also end in snake mouths. Those snake mouths were believed to hold deadly dragon venom. Her home was said to be either the Western Ocean or the waters by the Ethiopian abyss.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "meduza-russian-folklore-c02",
    "meduza-russian-folklore-c03",
    "meduza-russian-folklore-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia juga disebut dalam \"Kitab Ilmu Alam\" yang dikaitkan dengan Nikolai Spathari. Menurut B. M. Sokolov, kisah pada lubok ini misterius tetapi merujuk pada tema yang dikenal dari tradisi lisan.",
   "en": "She is also mentioned in the \"Book of Natural Science\" attributed to Nikolai Spathari. According to B. M. Sokolov, the subject of this lubok is mysterious but refers to a theme known from oral narrative.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "meduza-russian-folklore-c04"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "meduza-russian-folklore-c01",
   "source_id": "meduza-russian-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "Meduza (Russian: Медуза), Meluza (Russian: Мелуза, literally \"small\", \"little\") or Meluzina (Russian: Мелузина) is a mythical creature in Russian folklore. She was depicted in a Russian lubok of the 17th or 18th century. She is described as half-woman, half-snake, or as the half-woman, half-fish creature. She is also said to be the deity of deception.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Meduza (Медуза), Meluza (harfiah \"kecil\") atau Meluzina adalah makhluk mitis cerita rakyat Rusia yang digambarkan pada lubok Rusia abad ke-17 atau ke-18 sebagai setengah perempuan setengah ular atau ikan, dan disebut dewi tipu daya.",
    "en": "Meduza (Медуза), Meluza (literally \"little\") or Meluzina is a mythical creature of Russian folklore depicted in a 17th- or 18th-century Russian lubok as half woman, half snake or fish, and said to be the deity of deception."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "meduza-russian-folklore-c02",
   "source_id": "meduza-russian-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "She is represented as a sea monster with the head of a beautiful dark-haired maiden, having the body and belly of a striped beast, a dragon tail with a snake's mouth at the end, and legs resembling those of an elephant with the same snake mouths at the end. She also wears a crown.",
   "locator": "Appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai monster laut berkepala gadis cantik berambut gelap, bertubuh binatang belang, berekor naga dengan mulut ular di ujung, berkaki seperti gajah dengan mulut ular di ujungnya, dan bermahkota.",
    "en": "She is shown as a sea monster with a beautiful dark-haired maiden's head, a striped beast's body, a dragon tail ending in a snake mouth, elephant-like legs ending in snake mouths, and a crown."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "meduza-russian-folklore-c03",
   "source_id": "meduza-russian-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "According to belief, her snake mouths contained a deadly dragon poison. She was said to live in the Sea near the Ethiopian abyss, or in the Western Ocean.",
   "locator": "Appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut kepercayaan, mulut-mulut ularnya mengandung racun naga yang mematikan; ia tinggal di laut dekat jurang Ethiopia atau di Samudra Barat.",
    "en": "According to belief her snake mouths held deadly dragon poison; she lived in the sea near the Ethiopian abyss or in the Western Ocean."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "meduza-russian-folklore-c04",
   "source_id": "meduza-russian-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "Упоминается в «Книге естествословной», приписываемой Николаю Спафарию. По мнению Б. М. Соколова, сюжет этого лубка является загадочным, но содержит отсылку к знакомой теме из устного повествования.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia disebut dalam \"Kitab Ilmu Alam\" yang dikaitkan dengan Nikolai Spathari; menurut B. M. Sokolov, kisah lubok ini misterius tetapi merujuk tema yang dikenal dari tradisi lisan.",
    "en": "She is mentioned in the \"Book of Natural Science\" attributed to Nikolai Spathari; according to B. M. Sokolov, this lubok's subject is mysterious but refers to a theme known from oral narrative."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "meduza-russian-folklore-c05",
   "source_id": "meduza-russian-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "Мелуза (так же Медуза или Мелузина) — персонаж русских лубочных картинок второй половины XVII—XVIII века. Представляет собой мифическое существо в виде полуженщины-полузмеи или полурыбы.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Meluza (juga Meduza atau Meluzina) adalah tokoh gambar lubok Rusia dari paruh kedua abad ke-17 (XVII) hingga abad ke-18 (XVIII), makhluk mitis setengah perempuan setengah ular atau ikan.",
    "en": "Meluza (also Meduza or Meluzina) is a figure of Russian lubok pictures from the second half of the 17th (XVII) to the 18th (XVIII) century, a mythical being half woman, half snake or fish."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "meduza-russian-folklore-c06",
   "source_id": "meduza-russian-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "Змеиные пасти содержали в себе смертельный драконий яд. Обитала Мелуза в западном Океане, в «океане-море близ Эфиопской пучины».",
   "locator": "Внешний вид",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mulut-mulut ularnya berisi racun naga yang mematikan; Meluza tinggal di Samudra barat, \"di samudra-laut dekat jurang Ethiopia\".",
    "en": "Her snake mouths held deadly dragon venom; Meluza lived in the western Ocean, \"in the ocean-sea near the Ethiopian abyss\"."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {

   "searched": "Lubok Meluza mungkin ada di Commons, tetapi belum diperiksa pada putaran ini."
  }
 ]
}
```

```json
  "en": "Moryana is a female sea spirit of Slavic folklore, possibly a goddess, the Sea Tsar's daughter who rules the winds from the sea.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Moryana adalah roh laut perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Slavia, mungkin seorang dewi; A. M. Remizov menyebutnya dewi laut dan penguasa angin. Ia vodyanitsa laut dan putri Tsar Laut. Kadang moryany disebut sebagai banyak roh laut, semacam rusalka laut yang mengancam kapal, tetapi biasanya Moryana digambarkan sebagai satu sosok.",
   "en": "Slavic folklore tells of Moryana, a spirit of the sea who may once have been a goddess. Writer A. M. Remizov calls her goddess of the sea and mistress of the winds. She was a sea vodyanitsa and the Sea Tsar's daughter. At times the moryany were said to be many sea spirits, a marine kind of rusalka threatening ships, but Moryana was usually a single figure.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "moryana-c01",
    "moryana-c07"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Namanya berasal dari kata more, \"laut\", dengan akhiran perempuan -yana, kira-kira \"dia yang dari laut\". Di beberapa daerah Rusia namanya dipakai untuk angin dingin dan keras yang bertiup dari laut ke darat. Karena namanya mirip Morana, ia kadang disamakan dengan dewi itu dan disebut dewi kematian.",
   "en": "Her name comes from more, \"sea\", with the feminine ending -yana, roughly \"she of the sea\". In some Russian regions the name is used for the cold, harsh winds blowing from sea to land. Because her name sounds like Morana, she was sometimes identified with that goddess and called the goddess of death.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "moryana-c02",
    "moryana-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia dipercaya sering berenang jauh di laut dalam wujud ikan besar sambil bermain dengan lumba-lumba, dan hanya naik ke darat pada senja yang tenang. Bila Tsar Laut marah dan badai muncul, Moryana menenangkannya sehingga badai reda. Menurut kepercayaan lain, ia raksasa perempuan berwajah keras dengan rambut kusut dan berbaju putih yang menguasai angin tenggara di muara Volga, berbahaya bagi pelaut di Laut Kaspia; musuh utamanya Ded Shapka Dranaya, penguasa angin barat laut.",
   "en": "She was believed to swim deep in the sea as a big fish, playing with dolphins, and to come ashore only on quiet evenings. When the Sea Tsar grew angry and a storm rose, Moryana calmed him and the storm died down.  "short_description": {
```
```json
  "id": "Q'ursha adalah anjing legendaris dalam mitologi Georgia, paling dikenal sebagai teman setia pahlawan budaya Amirani.",
  "en": "Q'ursha is a legendary dog of Georgian mythology, best known as the faithful companion of the culture hero Amirani.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "q-ursha-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Q'ursha adalah anjing legendaris dalam mitologi Georgia. Ia muncul dalam berbagai cerita, tetapi paling terkenal sebagai teman setia Amirani. Namanya berarti \"telinga hitam\", nama anjing yang umum di Georgia. Ia dikatakan lahir dari burung gagak atau elang, sehingga kadang digambarkan bersayap elang, berkaki sangat besar, berbibir emas, dan bermata sebesar ayakan.",
   "en": "Q'ursha is a legendary dog of Georgian mythology. He features in many stories but is best remembered as Amirani's loyal friend. His name, a common Georgian dog name, means \"black-ear\". Born, it was said, of a raven or an eagle, he is sometimes shown with an eagle's wings, huge paws, golden lips and eyes the size of sieves.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "q-ursha-c01",
    "q-ursha-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia diberi kemampuan gaib: gonggongan seperti guntur, lompatan sejauh padang luas, dan kemampuan melacak buruan yang tak pernah gagal. Ketika Tuhan merantai Amirani di dalam gunung karena menantang-Nya, Q'ursha ikut terkurung dan terus menjilati rantai itu sampai hampir putus, tetapi setiap tahun rantainya diperbarui tepat sebelum Amirani bebas.",
   "en": "He was credited with uncanny powers: a bark like thunder, a leap the size of a great field and tracking that never failed. When God chained Amirani inside a mountain for his defiance, Q'ursha was shut in too and kept licking the chains until they were nearly worn through, but every year they were renewed just before Amirani could break free.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "q-ursha-c03",
    "q-ursha-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Q'ursha juga menjadi pokok lagu rakyat Georgia \"O my Kursha!\" yang setidaknya berasal dari abad ke-18; penyair Davit Guramishvili, yang lahir pada 1705, menulis bahwa ia ingin mendengarnya lagi. Setidaknya ada dua puluh tujuh versi lagu itu yang terdokumentasi. Ia juga disebut menemani pemburu Betkil.",
   "en": "Q'ursha is also the subject of the Georgian folk song \"O my Kursha!\", dating back to at least the 18th century; the poet Davit Guramishvili, born in 1705, wrote of wishing to hear it again. At least twenty-seven versions of the song are documented. He is also said to accompany the hunter Betkil.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "q-ursha-c05",
    "q-ursha-c06",
    "q-ursha-c07"
   ]
  }
 ],

   "quote": "is a legendary dog from Georgian mythology. Although he appears in a number of different stories, he is best known as the loyal companion of the culture hero Amirani. His name means \"black-ear\", a common Georgian name for dogs.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Q'ursha (ყურშა) adalah anjing legendaris mitologi Georgia, paling dikenal sebagai teman setia pahlawan budaya Amirani; namanya berarti \"telinga hitam\", nama anjing yang umum di Georgia.",
    "en": "Q'ursha is a legendary dog of Georgian mythology, best known as the loyal companion of the culture hero Amirani; his name means \"black-ear\", a common Georgian dog name."

  {
   "id": "q-ursha-c02",
   "source_id": "q-ursha-s1",
   "quote": "He was said to be born of either a raven or an eagle and is sometimes depicted as having eagle's wings as a result. Apart from his wings, Q'ursha was sometimes described with other special features: colossal paws, \"lips of gold, and eyes as big as sieves\".",

   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia dikatakan lahir dari gagak atau elang sehingga kadang digambarkan bersayap elang, dengan kaki sangat besar, bibir emas, dan mata sebesar ayakan.",

   }
  },
  {
   "id": "q-ursha-c03",
   "source_id": "q-ursha-s1",
   "quote": "He was attributed supernatural abilities such as a thunderous bark, a leap \"as big as a great field\" and an infallible ability to track prey.",

   }

  {

   "source_id": "q-ursha-s1",
```

```json
  "id": "Sânziană adalah peri lembut dalam cerita rakyat Rumania; bentuk jamaknya, Sânziene, juga nama perayaan tahunan untuk menghormati mereka pada 24 Juni.",
  "en": "Sânziană is a gentle fairy of Romanian folklore; the plural, Sânziene, also names a yearly festival in their honour on 24 June.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Sânziană adalah nama Rumania untuk peri-peri lembut yang penting dalam cerita rakyat setempat; kata itu juga dipakai untuk bunga Galium verum. Dalam bentuk jamak, Sânziene, kata ini menunjuk perayaan tahunan untuk menghormati para peri. Menurut Mircea Eliade, Sânziene berasal dari kultus Romawi bagi dewi Diana, Sancta Diana.",
   "en": "Sânziană is the Romanian word for gentle fairies who matter greatly in local lore, and it also names the Galium verum flower. In the plural, Sânziene, the word refers to a yearly feast held in the fairies' honour. Mircea Eliade traced the Sânziene to a Roman cult of the goddess Diana, Sancta Diana.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "sanziana-c01",
    "sanziana-c07"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Orang di Pegunungan Carpathia barat merayakan Sânziene setiap 24 Juni, yang dipercaya sebagai perayaan pagan titik balik matahari musim panas; menurut Gereja Ortodoks Rumania, adat itu berkaitan dengan kelahiran Santo Yohanes Pembaptis pada tanggal yang sama. Gadis-gadis tercantik desa berbaju putih memetik bunga, termasuk Galium verum, menganyam mahkota bunga, lalu menari mengelilingi api unggun.",
   "en": "People in the western Carpathians mark Sânziene every 24 June, thought to be a pagan summer-solstice rite; the Romanian Orthodox Church links the customs to the Nativity of Saint John the Baptist on that date. The village's loveliest girls, dressed in white, gather flowers including Galium verum, weave crowns and dance around a bonfire.",
   "claim_ids": [

  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi Rumania, Sânziene adalah peri baik dari golongan iele, tetapi bila hari raya mereka tidak dihormati mereka berubah menjadi seperti rusalii, peri jahat.    "en": "In Romanian mythology the Sânziene are good fairies of the iele class, but when their feast is not respected they turn into companions of the rusalii, evil fairies.     "id": "Orang di Pegunungan Carpathia barat merayakan Sânziene setiap 24 Juni, mirip Midsummer Swedia, dan dipercaya perayaan pagan titik balik matahari; menurut Gereja Ortodoks Rumania adat ini berkaitan dengan kelahiran Santo Yohanes Pembaptis.",
    "en": "People in the western Carpathians celebrate Sânziene each 24 June, similar to Swedish Midsummer and thought to be a pagan solstice feast; the Romanian Orthodox Church relates the customs to Saint John the Baptist's Nativity."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sanziana-c03",
   "source_id": "sanziana-s1",
   "quote": "The folk practices of Sânziene imply that the most beautiful maidens in the village dress in white and spend all day searching for and picking flowers, of which one MUST be Galium verum",
   "locator": "Sânziene rituals",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam adat Sânziene, gadis-gadis tercantik desa berbaju putih sepanjang hari mencari dan memetik bunga, salah satunya harus Galium verum, lalu menganyam mahkota bunga dan menari mengelilingi api unggun.",
    "en": "In Sânziene custom, the village's most beautiful maidens dress in white and pick flowers all day, one of which must be Galium verum, then weave crowns and dance around a bonfire."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sanziana-c04",
   "source_id": "sanziana-s2",
   "quote": "Sânzienele sunt, în mitologia românească, zâne bune din clasa ielelor, dar care atunci când nu le este respectată sărbătoarea devin surate cu rusaliile, care sunt zâne rele.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Rumania, Sânziene adalah peri baik dari golongan iele, tetapi bila hari rayanya tidak dihormati mereka menjadi sejenis rusalii, peri jahat.",
    "en": "In Romanian mythology the Sânziene are good fairies of the iele class, but when their feast is not respected they become kin to the rusalii, who are evil fairies."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sanziana-c05",
   "source_id": "sanziana-s2",
   "quote": "Uneori sânzienele sunt numite și drăgaice",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kadang Sânziene juga disebut drăgaice.",
    "en": "The Sânziene are sometimes also called drăgaice."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sanziana-c06",
   "source_id": "sanziana-s2",
   "quote": "în straturile mai recente ale folclorului apare și o zână individualizată, Iana Sânziana (sora Soarelui în colindele cosmologice)",
   "locator": "Origine și etimologie",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam lapisan cerita rakyat yang lebih baru muncul peri tunggal, Iana Sânziana, saudari Matahari dalam colinde kosmologis.",
    "en": "In more recent layers of folklore a single fairy appears, Iana Sânziana, sister of the Sun in cosmological carols."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sanziana-c07",
   "source_id": "sanziana-s2",
   "quote": "După Mircea Eliade, Sânzienele provin dintr-un cult roman, raportat la zeița Diana — Sancta Diana",
   "locator": "Origine și etimologie",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut Mircea Eliade, Sânziene berasal dari kultus Romawi yang berkaitan dengan dewi Diana, Sancta Diana.",
    "en": "According to Mircea Eliade, the Sânziene derive from a Roman cult connected with the goddess Diana, Sancta Diana."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {
   "field": "images",
   "searched": "Foto perayaan Sânziene mungkin ada di Commons tetapi belum diperiksa."
  }
 ]
}
```

```json
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Albania, shtriga adalah penyihir vampir yang malam-malam mengisap darah bayi yang sedang tidur, lalu menjelma serangga terbang, biasanya ngengat, lalat, atau lebah.    "en": "In Albanian folklore the shtriga is a vampire witch who drinks the blood of sleeping babies by night and then becomes a flying insect, usually a moth, fly or bee. Only the shtriga herself can heal her victims, often by spitting into their mouths; those left uncured fall ill and die.",
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Solomonar adalah penyihir dalam cerita rakyat Rumania yang dipercaya menunggang naga dan mengendalikan cuaca, mendatangkan hujan, guntur, atau badai es.",
  "en": "The solomonar is a wizard of Romanian folklore believed to ride a dragon and control the weather, bringing rain, thunder or hail.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "solomonari-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Rumania, solomonar (jamak solomonari) adalah penyihir yang menunggang naga, zmeu atau balaur, dan mengatur cuaca, mendatangkan hujan, guntur, atau badai es. Solomonari bukan makhluk gaib, melainkan manusia yang mempelajari sihir; mereka direkrut dari rakyat biasa dan belajar di sekolah sihir Solomonărie atau Şolomanţă.",
   "en": "In Romanian folklore the solomonar (plural solomonari) is a wizard who rides a dragon, a zmeu or balaur, and governs the weather, bringing rain, thunder or hailstorms. They are not supernatural beings but people who have learned sorcery; they are drawn from ordinary folk and trained at the magic school called Solomonărie or Şolomanţă.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "solomonari-c01",
    "solomonari-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Mereka digambarkan bertubuh tinggi, berambut merah, berjubah putih panjang seperti petani atau berpakaian compang-camping. Saat tidak melayani Iblis, mereka sering terlihat meminta-minta, dan uang yang terkumpul dilempar ke sungai sebagai persembahan bagi roh air. Tas sihir mereka berisi kapak besi, tali kekang dari kulit kayu birch atau kekang emas, dan sebuah kitab kebijaksanaan yang menjadi sumber kekuatan mereka.",
   "en": "They are pictured as tall and red-haired, in long white peasant robes or in rags. When not serving the Devil they are often seen begging, and the coins they collect are thrown into rivers as offerings to the water spirits. Their magic bag holds an iron axe, birch-bark reins or a golden bridle, and a book of wisdom that is the source of their power.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "solomonari-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Pada masa awal solomonari dianggap penyihir baik, tetapi setelah datangnya Kekristenan mereka berubah menjadi jahat. Nama mereka umumnya dikaitkan dengan Raja Salomo melalui akhiran pekerjaan -ar, walau itu mungkin etimologi rakyat.",
   "en": "Early on the solomonari were thought of as good magicians, but after Christianity arrived they turned evil. Their name is usually linked to King Solomon through the occupational suffix -ar, though this may be folk etymology.",

    "solomonari-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

   "title": "Соломонары",
   "author": null,

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "solomonari-c01",
   "source_id": "solomonari-s1",
   "quote": "is a wizard believed in Romanian folklore to ride a dragon (zmeu or a balaur) and control the weather, causing rain, thunder, or hailstorm.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Solomonar adalah penyihir yang dalam cerita rakyat Rumania dipercaya menunggang naga (zmeu atau balaur) dan mengendalikan cuaca, mendatangkan hujan, guntur, atau badai es; mereka direkrut dari rakyat biasa dan belajar di Solomonărie atau Şolomanţă.",
    "en": "The solomonar is a wizard believed in Romanian folklore to ride a dragon (zmeu or balaur) and control the weather, causing rain, thunder or hail; they are recruited from common folk and taught at the Solomonărie or Şolomanţă."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "solomonari-c02",
   "source_id": "solomonari-s2",
   "quote": "Соломонары не являются сверхъестественными существами. Это люди, выучившиеся колдовству.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Solomonari bukan makhluk gaib, melainkan manusia yang mempelajari sihir.",
    "en": "The solomonari are not supernatural beings; they are people who have learned sorcery."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "solomonari-c03",
   "source_id": "solomonari-s1",
   "quote": "The Solomonars are said to be tall, red-haired, wearing long white robes of peasants, sometimes woolen, or clad in ragged attire made from patches",
   "locator": "General description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Solomonar dikatakan tinggi, berambut merah, berjubah putih panjang petani atau berpakaian compang-camping; saat tidak melayani Iblis mereka meminta-minta dan melempar uangnya ke sungai bagi roh air; tas sihir mereka berisi kapak besi, kekang, dan kitab kebijaksanaan sumber kekuatan mereka.",
    "en": "Solomonars are said to be tall, red-haired, in long white peasant robes or ragged patched attire; when not helping the Devil they beg and throw the coins into rivers for the water spirits; their magic bag holds an iron axe, reins and a book of wisdom, the source of their power."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "solomonari-c04",
   "source_id": "solomonari-s1",
   "quote": "They are taught their magic and the speech of beasts at the school (Scholomance), and become capable of riding the dragons.",
   "locator": "Schooling",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka belajar sihir dan bahasa binatang di sekolah Scholomance dan menjadi mampu menunggang naga.",
    "en": "They learn magic and the speech of beasts at the Scholomance and become able to ride dragons."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "solomonari-c05",
   "source_id": "solomonari-s1",
   "quote": "The default explanation is that the word is connected to King Solomon via the addition of the occupational suffix \"-ar\", although this may be folk etymology.",
   "locator": "Etymologies",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Penjelasan umum adalah bahwa kata ini berkaitan dengan Raja Salomo (King Solomon) lewat akhiran pekerjaan -ar, walau mungkin etimologi rakyat.",
    "en": "The default explanation links the word to King Solomon via the occupational suffix -ar, though this may be folk etymology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "solomonari-c06",
   "source_id": "solomonari-s2",
   "quote": "На раннем этапе истории соломонары считались добрыми магами, но с приходом христианства они превратились в злых.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada masa awal solomonari dianggap penyihir baik, tetapi dengan datangnya Kekristenan mereka menjadi jahat.",
    "en": "Early on the solomonari were considered good magicians, but with the coming of Christianity they turned evil."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
```

```json
 "traits": [],
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Stuhać adalah makhluk iblis dalam mitologi Serbia dari Herzegovina yang tinggal di pegunungan tinggi dan memakai pengikat dari urat manusia di kakinya.",
  "en": "Stuhać is a demonic creature of Serbian mythology from Herzegovina that lives in high mountains and wears ties made of human sinews on its feet.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "stuhac-c01",
   "stuhac-c02"
  ]

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Stuhać adalah makhluk mitis bersifat iblis dalam mitologi Serbia yang tercatat di Herzegovina. Walau namanya mirip zduhać, keduanya sama sekali tidak serupa. Dalam cerita rakyat Serbia ia disebut \"iblis atau vila\".",
   "en": "Stuhać is a demonic mythical being of Serbian mythology recorded in Herzegovina. Although its name resembles zduhać, the two have nothing in common. Serbian folklore calls it \"a devil or a vila\".",
   "claim_ids": [
    "stuhac-c01",
    "stuhac-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia tinggal di pegunungan tinggi dan tanah tandus. Rupanya tidak pernah dijelaskan; yang diketahui hanyalah ia memakai pengikat kaki dari urat manusia agar tidak tergelincir di tebing gunung. Bila pengikatnya rusak, ia menarik urat dari kaki seseorang untuk membuat yang baru. Untuk mengusirnya, orang dipercaya harus mengangkat kaki setinggi tangan agar ia bingung.",
   "en": "It dwells in high mountains and barren places. Its looks are never described; all that is told is that it binds its feet with ties of human sinew so as not to slip on mountain cliffs. When they wear out, it pulls sinews from someone's legs to make new ones. To scare it off, people believed one must raise the legs to the level of the hands to confuse it.",
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Tugarin adalah makhluk mitis dalam bylina dan dongeng Rusia yang melambangkan kejahatan dan kekejaman, berwujud mirip naga, dan menjadi lawan Alyosha Popovich.",
  "en": "Tugarin is a mythical being of Russian byliny and fairy tales who embodies evil and cruelty, takes a dragon-like form and opposes Alyosha Popovich.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "tugarin-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam bylina dan dongeng Rusia, Tugarin adalah sosok kejam dan jahat yang tampil menyerupai naga. Ia digambarkan sebagai raksasa dan musuh Alyosha Popovich, juga lawan Dobrynya Nikitich. Namanya beragam, antara lain Zmey Tugarin, Zmey Tugaretin, dan Zmeishche Tugarishche.",
   "en": "In Russian byliny and tales Tugarin is a cruel, wicked figure who appears in the likeness of a dragon. He is pictured as a giant and as the foe of Alyosha Popovich, and also of Dobrynya Nikitich. He goes by many names, among them Zmey Tugarin, Zmey Tugaretin and Zmeishche Tugarishche.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "tugarin-c01",
    "tugarin-c02",
    "tugarin-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Walau disebut naga, ia sebagian besar tetap berwujud manusia dan menunggang kuda. Ia tampak bersayap dan terbang ke udara saat bertarung dengan Alyosha, meski beberapa versi menjelaskan sayap itu sebagai alat dari kertas yang dipasang pada kudanya. Ia juga sangat rakus: mampu menjejalkan satu roti bundar di tiap pipinya lalu menelan seekor angsa utuh.",
   "en": "Though called a dragon, he mostly keeps a human shape and rides a horse. He seems to have wings and rises into the air while fighting Alyosha, though some versions explain the wings away as a paper device fixed to his horse. He is also a huge glutton, able to cram a whole round loaf into each cheek and then swallow a swan whole.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "tugarin-c03",
    "tugarin-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sebagian pengulas menganggap Tugarin melambangkan unsur api, karena dalam beberapa versi tubuhnya diselimuti ular berapi yang ia pakai sebagai senjata, dan ia mencoba mencekik Alyosha dengan asap. Namanya dikaitkan dengan khan Polovtsia Tugorkan, walau ada etimologi lain dari akar kata tug, \"duka\".",
   "en": "Some commentators see Tugarin as the element of fire, since in some versions his body is covered with fiery snakes used as weapons and he tries to choke Alyosha with smoke. His name has been tied to the Polovtsian khan Tugorkan, though another etymology derives it from the root tug, \"grief\".",
   "claim_ids": [
    "tugarin-c05",
    "tugarin-c07"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "tugarin-c01",
   "source_id": "tugarin-s1",
   "quote": "is a mythical creature in Russian byliny and fairy tales, which personifies evil and cruelty and appears in a dragon-like form. Tugarin is depicted as a giant and an opponent of Alyosha Popovich.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tugarin (Тугарин) adalah makhluk mitis dalam bylina dan dongeng Rusia yang mempersonifikasikan kejahatan dan kekejaman, berwujud mirip naga, digambarkan sebagai raksasa dan lawan Alyosha Popovich.",
    "en": "Tugarin is a mythical creature of Russian byliny and fairy tales personifying evil and cruelty, dragon-like in form, depicted as a giant and opponent of Alyosha Popovich."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tugarin-c02",
   "source_id": "tugarin-s1",
   "quote": "Tugarin has many different names in Russian byliny and fairy tales, such as Zmey Tugarin, Zmey Tugaretin, Zmeishche Tugarishche, and others.",
   "locator": "Name forms and origins",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tugarin punya banyak nama dalam bylina dan dongeng Rusia, seperti Zmey Tugarin, Zmey Tugaretin, dan Zmeishche Tugarishche.",
    "en": "Tugarin has many names in Russian byliny and tales, such as Zmey Tugarin, Zmey Tugaretin and Zmeishche Tugarishche."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tugarin-c03",
   "source_id": "tugarin-s1",
   "quote": "Tugarin, although spoken of as a dragon, largely retains an \"anthropomorphic\" form, and is seen riding a horse. It appears he has wings with which to fly, and he soars to the air during the duel with Alyosha.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Walau disebut naga, Tugarin sebagian besar berwujud manusia dan menunggang kuda; ia tampak bersayap dan terbang saat duel dengan Alyosha; beberapa versi menyebut sayap itu alat dari kertas yang dipasang pada kudanya.",
    "en": "Though called a dragon, Tugarin largely keeps a human form and rides a horse; he seems winged and soars during the duel with Alyosha; some versions call the wings a paper contraption on his horse."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tugarin-c04",
   "source_id": "tugarin-s1",
   "quote": "Tugarin was a great glutton, which is somewhat of a dragon-like trait. He could stuff a whole wheel of bread in each cheek, then deposit a whole swan on his tongue and swallow it.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tugarin sangat rakus: ia dapat menjejalkan satu roti bundar utuh di tiap pipi, lalu meletakkan seekor angsa utuh di lidahnya dan menelannya.",
    "en": "Tugarin was a great glutton: he could stuff a whole wheel of bread in each cheek, then put a whole swan on his tongue and swallow it."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tugarin-c05",
   "source_id": "tugarin-s1",
   "quote": "It has been suggested by some commentators that Tugarin represents the element of fire, since in some versions of \"Alyosha Popovich\", Tugarin's torso is covered with fiery snakes which he uses as a weapon, attempts to strangle Alyosha with smoke",
   "locator": "Nature myth",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagian pengulas berpendapat Tugarin melambangkan unsur api, karena dalam beberapa versi tubuhnya diselimuti ular berapi yang dipakai sebagai senjata dan ia mencoba mencekik Alyosha dengan asap.",
    "en": "Some commentators suggest Tugarin represents fire, since in some versions his torso is covered with fiery snakes used as weapons and he tries to strangle Alyosha with smoke."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tugarin-c06",
   "source_id": "tugarin-s2",
   "quote": "(также Змей Тугарин, Тугарин Змей, Змей Тугаретин, Тугарин Змеевич, Змеище Тугарище) — злой богатырь в русских былинах и сказках, противник русских богатырей Добрыни Никитича и Алёши Поповича.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tugarin (juga Zmey Tugarin, Tugarin Zmeyevich, dan lain-lain) adalah bogatyr jahat dalam bylina dan dongeng Rusia, lawan para bogatyr Rusia Dobrynya Nikitich dan Alyosha Popovich.",
    "en": "Tugarin (also Zmey Tugarin, Tugarin Zmeyevich and others) is an evil bogatyr of Russian byliny and tales, opponent of the Russian bogatyrs Dobrynya Nikitich and Alyosha Popovich."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tugarin-c07",
   "source_id": "tugarin-s1",
   "quote": "The name Tugarin as a corruption of Tugar-Khan (Tugor-Khan ... of the Turkic Polovets), has been contested by an etymology from the root tug \"grief\".",
   "locator": "Name forms and origins",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Nama Tugarin sebagai rusakan dari Tugar-Khan (Tugor-Khan dari bangsa Turkik Polovtsia) dibantah oleh etimologi dari akar kata tug, \"duka\".",
    "en": "The name Tugarin as a corruption of Tugar-Khan (Tugor-Khan of the Turkic Polovtsians) has been contested by an etymology from the root tug, \"grief\"."
   }
  },
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Vjesci (wieszczy) adalah vampir dalam cerita rakyat Polandia; orang yang ditakdirkan menjadi vjesci lahir dengan selaput ketuban di kepala.",
  "en": "The vjesci (wieszczy) is a vampire of Polish folklore; those fated to become one are born with a caul on the head.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "vjesci-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Vjesci, dalam bahasa Polandia wieszczy, adalah vampir dalam cerita rakyat Polandia. Menurut legenda, sebagian orang lahir dengan takdir menjadi vjesci, yang tampak dari selaput ketuban di kepala bayi. Untuk mencegahnya, selaput itu dilepas, dikeringkan, digiling, lalu diberikan kepada anak itu pada ulang tahunnya yang ketujuh. Dalam demonologi Slavia, wieszczy adalah bentuk keberadaan jiwa manusia setelah mati, mirip upiór atau vampir, tetapi di banyak daerah punya ciri tersendiri.",
   "en": "Called wieszczy in Polish, the vjesci is a vampire of Polish lore. Legend says some people are born destined for it, marked by a caul on the newborn's head. To prevent this, the caul was taken off, dried, ground and given to the child on their seventh birthday. In Slavic demonology the wieszczy is a form of the soul's existence after death, close to the upiór or vampire yet with its own traits in many regions.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vjesci-c01",
    "vjesci-c02",
    "vjesci-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Semasa hidup, vjesci biasanya tidak bisa dibedakan dari manusia. Saat meninggal ia menolak sakramen; tubuhnya cepat dingin, anggota badannya tetap lentur, dan bibir serta pipinya tetap merah. Menurut legenda ia bangkit pada tengah malam setelah dikubur, memakan pakaian dan sebagian dagingnya sendiri, lalu pulang untuk memangsa keluarga dan tetangga, kemudian membunyikan lonceng gereja; yang mendengarnya akan menjadi korban berikutnya.",
   "en": "In life a vjesci could rarely be told apart from other people. At death it refuses the sacrament; the body cools fast, the limbs stay supple and the lips and cheeks stay red. Legend has it rise at midnight after burial, eat its clothes and some of its own flesh, go home to prey on family and neighbours, then ring the church bell; whoever hears it will be the next victim.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vjesci-c02",
    "vjesci-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Untuk mencegah serangan, orang yang sekarat harus menerima Ekaristi, tanah ditaruh di dalam peti, dan salib atau koin diletakkan di bawah lidah jenazah agar diisap sang vampir. Jenazah juga dibaringkan telungkup agar, bila bangkit, ia justru menggali lebih dalam ke tanah.",
   "en": "To prevent attacks, the dying had to receive the Eucharist, soil was put in the coffin, and a crucifix or coin was laid under the corpse's tongue for the vampire to suck on. Bodies were also laid face down so that, if they woke, they would only dig deeper into the earth.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vjesci-c04",
    "vjesci-c05"
   ]
  }
 ],

   "source_id": "vjesci-s1",
   "quote": "Typically, vjesci were said to be indistinguishable from humans, although in some stories, they had a ruddy complexion and an excitable nature.    "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vjesci biasanya tak bisa dibedakan dari manusia; saat mati ia menolak sakramen, tubuhnya cepat dingin, anggota badan tetap lentur, dan bibir serta pipinya tetap merah.",
    "en": "Vjesci were usually indistinguishable from humans; at death they refused the sacrament, the body cooled quickly, the limbs stayed limber and lips and cheeks stayed red."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vjesci-c03",
   "source_id": "vjesci-s1",
   "quote": "the vjesci did not die, instead returning to life at midnight after its burial and eating its clothes and some of its own flesh. The vampire would leave the grave and return home to eat its family and neighbors. After visiting its relatives, it would go to the local church and ring the church bell. Those who heard the bell were supposedly destined to be the vampire's next victims.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vjesci tidak mati, melainkan bangkit pada tengah malam setelah dikubur, memakan pakaian dan sebagian dagingnya, meninggalkan kubur untuk memangsa keluarga dan tetangga, lalu membunyikan lonceng gereja; yang mendengarnya menjadi korban berikutnya.",
    "en": "The vjesci did not die but rose at midnight after burial, ate its clothes and some of its flesh, left the grave to eat family and neighbours, then rang the church bell; those who heard it were its next victims."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vjesci-c04",
   "source_id": "vjesci-s1",
   "quote": "All dying persons must receive the Eucharist. ... Soil was placed inside the coffin and underneath the body to prevent it from returning home.",
   "locator": "Preventing an attack",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Semua orang yang sekarat harus menerima Ekaristi; tanah ditaruh di dalam peti dan di bawah jenazah agar tidak pulang.",
    "en": "All dying persons must receive the Eucharist; soil was placed in the coffin and under the body to stop it returning home."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vjesci-c05",
   "source_id": "vjesci-s1",
   "quote": "A crucifix or coin was placed under the tongue of the corpse for the vampire to suck on. ... Bodies were laid in the coffin face down in belief that the corpse, if it returned to life, would simply dig further into the earth.",
   "locator": "Preventing an attack",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Salib atau koin diletakkan di bawah lidah jenazah agar diisap sang vampir; jenazah dibaringkan telungkup agar menggali lebih dalam bila bangkit.",
    "en": "A crucifix or coin was put under the corpse's tongue for the vampire to suck on; bodies were laid face down so that, if revived, they would dig deeper."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vjesci-c06",
   "source_id": "vjesci-s2",
   "quote": "postać z zakresu demonologii słowiańskiej, będąca formą pośmiertnej egzystencji ludzkiej duszy, zbliżona do wyobrażeń upiora lub wampira, posiadająca jednak na wielu terenach własną, odrębną tożsamość",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Wieszczy adalah tokoh demonologi Slavia, bentuk keberadaan jiwa manusia setelah mati, mirip upiór atau vampir, tetapi di banyak daerah punya identitas tersendiri.",
    "en": "The wieszczy is a figure of Slavic demonology, a form of the human soul's existence after death, close to the upiór or vampire but with its own identity in many regions."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {
   "field": "images",
   "searched": "Tidak ada gambar Commons yang terbukti menggambarkan vjesci."
  }
 ]
}
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Zburător (\"yang terbang\") adalah makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat Rumania, roh pengembara yang mendatangi gadis-gadis pada malam hari dalam rupa pemuda tampan.",
  "en": "The zburător (\"flyer\") is a supernatural being of Romanian folklore, a roving spirit who visits maidens by night in the guise of a handsome youth.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "zburator-c01",
   "zburator-c02"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Zburător atau sburător, yang berarti \"yang terbang\", adalah makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat Rumania, digambarkan sebagai roh pengembara yang bercinta dengan gadis-gadis pada malam hari. Ia disamakan dengan inkubus: iblis jahat yang mendatangi perempuan dalam mimpi dengan menyamar sebagai pemuda tampan. Di beberapa daerah ia disebut zmeu, atau dianggap salah satu wujud zmeu.",
   "en": "Known as zburător or sburător, \"the flyer\", this Romanian folk being is pictured as a wandering spirit who makes love to girls at night. He is compared to an incubus, a malicious demon that comes to women in dreams disguised as a handsome young man. In some regions he is called a zmeu, or seen as one of the shapes a zmeu can take.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "zburator-c01",
    "zburator-c02",
    "zburator-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dimitrie Cantemir menulis tentangnya dalam Descriptio Moldaviae (1714–1716): menurut kepercayaan orang Moldavia, ia hantu berupa pemuda tampan yang datang tengah malam kepada perempuan, terutama pengantin baru, dan tidak dapat dilihat orang lain. Penyair Ion Heliade Rădulescu mengolah mitos ini dalam puisi Zburătorul (1843), dan George Călinescu menggolongkannya sebagai salah satu dari empat mitos dasar puisi rakyat Rumania.",
   "en": "Dimitrie Cantemir wrote of him in Descriptio Moldaviae (1714–1716): Moldavians believed he was a ghost in the form of a handsome youth who came at midnight to women, especially newlyweds, unseen by anyone else. The poet Ion Heliade Rădulescu reworked the myth in the poem Zburătorul (1843), and George Călinescu counted it among the four fundamental myths of Romanian folk poetry.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "zburator-c04",
    "zburator-c05",
    "zburator-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],
```

```json
  "id": "E Bukura e Dheut (\"Kecantikan Bumi\") adalah julukan dalam mitologi Albania bagi peri yang licik atau, dalam tradisi lain, dewi bumi dan dunia bawah.",
  "en": "E Bukura e Dheut (\"Beauty of the Earth\") is an Albanian mythological epithet for a crafty fairy or, in other traditions, an earth and underworld goddess.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "e-bukura-e-dheut-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "E Bukura e Dheut, \"Kecantikan Bumi\", adalah julukan dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Albania. Dalam sebagian tradisi ia peri yang licik, dalam tradisi lain dewi bumi atau dunia bawah, pasangan e Bukura e Detit (Kecantikan Laut) dan i Bukuri i Qiellit (Kecantikan Langit). Dalam cerita rakyat Albania tengah ia saudari e Bukura e Detit dan istri Baba Tomor.",
   "en": "E Bukura e Dheut, \"Beauty of the Earth\", is an epithet of Albanian myth and folklore. Some traditions make her a cunning fairy, others a goddess of the earth or underworld, matched with e Bukura e Detit (Beauty of the Sea) and i Bukuri i Qiellit (Beauty of the Sky). Central Albanian lore makes her sister of e Bukura e Detit and wife of Baba Tomor.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "e-bukura-e-dheut-c01",
    "e-bukura-e-dheut-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia adalah kecantikan yang menjelma, bercahaya dengan rambut keemasan. Ia bisa roh baik, tetapi lebih sering jahat, dengan kekuatan sihir yang berasal dari pakaiannya. Ia tinggal di dunia bawah, di istana yang dijaga anjing berkepala tiga, kuçedra, dan berbagai makhluk aneh. Mencari atau menyelamatkannya, bahkan sampai ke istananya di dunia bawah, adalah motif yang sangat populer dalam dongeng Albania.",
   "en": "She is beauty in visible form, shining with golden hair. She can be a kindly spirit but is more often wicked, drawing her magic from her dress. She lives in the underworld in a palace guarded by a three-headed dog, a kuçedra and many strange creatures. A prince's quest to find or rescue her, even in her underground palace, is a favourite motif of Albanian tales.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "e-bukura-e-dheut-c03",
    "e-bukura-e-dheut-c04",
    "e-bukura-e-dheut-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

 ],
 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-c01",
   "source_id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-s1",
   "quote": "(the \"Beauty of the Earth\" or \"Earthly Beauty\") is an epithet in Albanian mythology and folklore, used in some traditions for a crafty fairy, and in other traditions for a chthonic/earth goddess, the counterpart of e Bukura e Detit (the Beauty of the Sea) and i Bukuri i Qiellit (the Beauty of the Sky).",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "E Bukura e Dheut (\"Kecantikan Bumi\") adalah julukan dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Albania bagi peri licik dalam sebagian tradisi dan dewi bumi atau dunia bawah dalam tradisi lain, pasangan e Bukura e Detit (Kecantikan Laut) dan i Bukuri i Qiellit (Kecantikan Langit).",
    "en": "E Bukura e Dheut (\"Beauty of the Earth\") is an epithet in Albanian myth and folklore for a crafty fairy in some traditions and a chthonic or earth goddess in others, counterpart of e Bukura e Detit (Beauty of the Sea) and i Bukuri i Qiellit (Beauty of the Sky)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-c02",
   "source_id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-s1",
   "quote": "In central Albanian folklore E Bukura e Dheut appears as the sister of e Bukura e Detit and the consort of Baba Tomor.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Albania tengah, E Bukura e Dheut adalah saudari e Bukura e Detit dan pasangan Baba Tomor.",
    "en": "In central Albanian folklore E Bukura e Dheut is the sister of e Bukura e Detit and consort of Baba Tomor."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-c03",
   "source_id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-s1",
   "quote": "E Bukura e Dheut is beauty made manifest, radiating with golden hair. She may be a good spirit or (more often) evil, with magical powers that derive from her dress, and lives in the underworld, where her palace is guarded by a three-headed dog, a kuçedra and all sorts of other weird and wonderful creatures.",
   "locator": "Role",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia kecantikan yang menjelma, bercahaya dengan rambut emas; bisa roh baik atau (lebih sering) jahat, kekuatan sihirnya dari pakaiannya, dan tinggal di dunia bawah dalam istana yang dijaga anjing berkepala tiga, kuçedra, dan makhluk aneh lainnya.",
    "en": "She is beauty made manifest with golden hair; a good or (more often) evil spirit whose magic comes from her dress, living in the underworld in a palace guarded by a three-headed dog, a kuçedra and other strange creatures."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-c04",
   "source_id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-s1",
   "quote": "The quest for the e Bukura e Dheut is a very popular and frequent motif in Albanian folktales: the princely hero must search for or rescue the Earthly Beauty, even going into her mystical underworld palace.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pencarian e Bukura e Dheut adalah motif sangat populer dalam dongeng Albania: pahlawan pangeran harus mencari atau menyelamatkannya, bahkan masuk ke istana mistisnya di dunia bawah.",
    "en": "The quest for e Bukura e Dheut is a very popular motif in Albanian folktales: the princely hero must find or rescue her, even entering her mystical underworld palace."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-c05",
   "source_id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-s2",
   "quote": "Në folklorin qendror shqiptar, E Bukura e Dheut është gruaja e perëndisë Tomor.",
   "locator": "Referime",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Albania tengah, E Bukura e Dheut adalah istri dewa Tomor.",
    "en": "In central Albanian folklore, E Bukura e Dheut is the wife of the god Tomor."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-c06",
   "source_id": "e-bukura-e-dheut-s2",
   "quote": "Kërkimi për E Bukura e Dheut është një motiv shumë i popullarizuar dhe i shpeshtë në tregimet folklorike shqiptare",
   "locator": "Referime",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pencarian E Bukura e Dheut adalah motif yang sangat populer dan sering dalam cerita rakyat Albania.",
    "en": "The search for E Bukura e Dheut is a very popular and frequent motif in Albanian folk tales."
   }
  }
 ],
```
